  ⎿  Created docs/runbooks/reconciliation.md (+67 -0)
       1 +# Runbook: reconciliation                                      
       2 +                                                               
       3 +`npm run reconcile` checks that the ledger and the cached balan
         +ces agree (section 1.5 of [spec 002](../../specs/002-ledger/spe
         +c.md), LED-R19 to LED-R22). It only reads: it is safe to run at
         + any time, against any database, while the service is serving. 
       4 +                                                               
       5 +## When to run it                                              
       6 +                                                               
       7 +- After every integration test run: CI runs it against the test
         + database, and a drift fails the build (LED-R22).              
       8 +- After an incident that touched the database: a failed migrati
         +on, a manual fix, a restore from backup.                       
       9 +- Whenever a balance looks wrong to a customer or an operator. 
      10 +                                                               
      11 +## How to run it                                               
      12 +                                                               
      13 +```sh                                                          
      14 +DATABASE_URL=postgres://scf_app:...@host:5432/scf npm run recon
         +cile                                                           
      15 +```                                                            
      16 +                                                               
      17 +Locally, `DATABASE_URL` comes from `.env`, so `npm run reconcil
         +e` checks the development database. Use the runtime role `scf_a
         +pp`, as the service does; the command needs no other grant.    
      18 +                                                               
      19 +It runs one read-only `REPEATABLE READ` transaction: a single s
         +napshot with no row locks, so it blocks no movement and sees ev
         +ery committed movement or none of it. Its statement timeout is 
         +600000 ms (10 minutes, SEC-R48). It never prints the URL or a d
         +river message, which may hold credentials; a failure names only
         + its SQLSTATE.                                                 
      20 +                                                               
      21 +## Reading the report                                          
      22 +                                                               
      23 +The command writes one JSON object on stdout. Every amount is a
         + string of decimal digits in minor units of its currency.      
      24 +                                                               
      25 +```json                                                        
      26 +{                                                              
      27 +  "discrepancies": [                                           
      28 +    {                                                          
      29 +      "accountId": "0192f0a0-...",                             
      30 +      "currency": "EUR",                                       
      31 +      "cachedBalance": "1000",                                 
      32 +      "entriesSum": "900",                                     
      33 +      "difference": "100"                                      
      34 +    }                                                          
      35 +  ],                                                           
      36 +  "totals": [                                                  
      37 +    { "currency": "USD", "sum": "0" },                         
      38 +    { "currency": "MXN", "sum": "0" },                         
      39 +    { "currency": "EUR", "sum": "100" },

      55  ## What to do on drift (exit 1)
      56  
      57 -The ledger entries are the source of truth; a cached balance is
         - derived from them (ADR-0006, ADR-0007).                       
      57 +The ledger entries are the source of truth; a cached balance is
         + derived from them (ADR-0006, ADR-0007). Every movement writes 
         +its entries and the balance change in one database transaction,
         + so a drift is a defect, not a race.                           
      58  
      59 -1. Do not edit the ledger: it is append-only, and the database 
         -refuses updates and deletes of entries (LED-R11).              
      60 -2. Keep the report, and find the account's last movements: its 
         -history (`GET /v1/accounts/{id}/entries`) and the audit records
         - naming it.                                                    
      61 -3. A discrepancy with every total at "0" except in the same cur
         -rency is a cached balance that drifted. Find the cause before c
         -orrecting anything: a code path that changed a balance without 
         -an entry is a defect.                                          
      62 -4. Correct money through the API only, with a compensating move
         -ment or a reversal, never with SQL on `accounts.balance` or `le
         -dger_entries`.                                                 
      63 -5. Run `npm run reconcile` again until it exits 0.             
      59 +1. Keep the report. Do not edit the ledger: it is append-only, 
         +and the database refuses updates and deletes of transactions an
         +d entries (SYS-R15, LED-R16).                                  
      60 +2. For each account listed, read its history (`GET /v1/accounts
         +/{id}/entries`) and the audit records that name it, to find the
         + last movements before the drift.                              
      61 +3. A total that is not "0" in a currency with no discrepancy po
         +ints at a system account's entries; one that matches a discrepa
         +ncy is that cached balance.                                    
      62 +4. Treat it as an incident and escalate to the service owner. T
         +he service has no supported procedure for rewriting a cached ba
         +lance, and a deposit, withdrawal or reversal changes the entrie
         +s and the balance together, so it cannot remove a drift.

  ⎿  Updated README.md (+20 -0)
      19  ```
      20  
      21  Every behaviour is specified before it is built: the specs, wit
          h their requirements and acceptance criteria, are in [`specs/`]
          (specs/README.md), and `npm run trace` fails when an acceptance
           criterion that must be proven has no passing test.
      22 +                                                               
      23 +## API                                                         
      24 +                                                               
      25 +Every endpoint is served under `/v1` and needs `Authorization: 
         +Bearer <JWT>` with the role `customer` or `operator` (`npm run 
         +token -- --sub <uuid> --role customer` prints one for local use
         +). Swagger UI is at `/docs` and the OpenAPI document at `/docs/
         +json`, without credentials. Every error is `application/problem
         ++json` (RFC 9457), and every response carries an `X-Request-Id`
         +.                                                              
      26 +                                                               
      27 +## Accounts                                                    
      28 +                                                               
      29 +A customer opens accounts in one of USD, MXN, EUR, COP or JPY (
         +`POST /v1/accounts`), lists their own (`GET /v1/accounts`) and 
         +reads each one with its balance and history (`GET /v1/accounts/
         +{id}`, `GET /v1/accounts/{id}/entries`). An operator reads any 
         +customer account by id, with its `ownerId`, but lists none. Ano
         +ther customer's account, a system account and an unknown id all
         + answer the same 404.                                          
      30 +                                                               
      31 +Lists go newest first, `limit` from 1 to 100 (20 by default), a
         +nd continue with the opaque `nextCursor`, which is signed with 
         +`CURSOR_SECRET`, accepted by every replica, and valid only for 
         +the list and user it was issued to.                            
      32 +                                                               
      33 +An account is `active`, `frozen` or `closed`. Operators freeze,
         + unfreeze and close accounts (`POST /v1/accounts/{id}/freeze`, 
         +`/unfreeze`, `/close`); only an account with balance "0" can be
         + closed, and `closed` is final. A frozen or closed account take
         +s no deposit, withdrawal or transfer on either side; a reversal
         + is still allowed on a frozen account. Spec: [`specs/001-accoun
         +ts`](specs/001-accounts/spec.md).                              
      34 +                                                               
      35 +## Ledger                                                      
      36 +                                                               
      37 +Every money movement is one transaction of two or more ledger e
         +ntries that sum to zero, in one currency, written in the same d
         +atabase transaction as the balance changes. Amounts are integer
         +s in minor units of the currency: `bigint` in the code, strings
         + of decimal digits such as `"1050"` (10.50 EUR) in the API, and
         + whole yen for JPY. An entry's amount is signed: positive adds 
         +to the account's balance, negative subtracts. One movement is a
         +t most `MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR`.                                     
      38 +                                                               
      39 +Deposits and withdrawals move money against the settlement acco
         +unt of their currency, a system account with no cached balance:
         + its balance is the sum of its entries and may go below zero. A
         + customer balance never does. The ledger is append-only, in the
         + code and in the database; a mistake is corrected by a reversal
         + (`POST /v1/transactions/{id}/reversals`), never by an edit.   
      40 +                                                               
      41 +`npm run reconcile` checks every cached balance against the led
         +ger and every currency's global sum against zero; see the [reco
         +nciliation runbook](docs/runbooks/reconciliation.md). Spec: [`s
         +pecs/002-ledger`](specs/002-ledger/spec.md).

  unit tests), npm run test:integration passes (109 files, 272 tests), and npm

  What changed
  - Tests: about 60 new integration test files, one or more per AC, covering the
    SYS ACs and the remaining HTTP ACs of specs 001 to 007. I wrote the SYS
    ACs, LED-AC23, AUT-AC02 and SEC-AC34 myself; background agents wrote the
    rest, one per spec.
  - Production code:
    - src/platform/http/request-id.ts: every response now carries X-Request-Id.
      A valid client value is kept; otherwise a UUIDv7 is generated, including
      on router and HTTP-parser errors.
    - Fastify's JSON body parse errors now answer 400
      /problems/malformed-request. They answered 500 before.
    - app.testSeams and app.attachedTestHooks() exist, so SYS-AC24 can check
      that the production app has no test seams.
    - The fault seam can now write one ledger entry with a different amount,
      which LED-AC23 needs.
    - /docs and /docs/json are served from src/platform/http/docs.ts. The
      OpenAPI document describes every route, minor units, signed entry amounts,
      MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR, the problem details schema, every problem type and the
      /v1 prefix. It passes redocly lint with one warning, for a missing
      license.
  - Docs: README sections on the API, accounts and the ledger, and a new
    docs/runbooks/reconciliation.md. I appended "Done" lines to the follow-ups
    of ADR-0006, 0007, 0018 and 0016, and added ADR-0004 and ADR-0010 to the
    Related ADRs of spec 001.

  Where the plans differ from your prompt
  - These pending ACs belong to 09-hardening, not 08-api, according to their
    plans: SYS-AC02, SYS-AC29, LED-AC15, MOV-AC14, MOV-AC19, REV-AC23 and
    IDM-AC10 to AC13. No 08-api task names them, so I left them. The docs tasks
    of specs 003 and 005 are also listed under 09-hardening.
  - MOV-AC01 and REV-AC01 write Location: /transactions/<id>. The tests check
    for /v1/transactions/<id>, because SYS-R43 says paths in the specs leave out
    the prefix.

  Worth knowing
  - MOV-AC03 can't be read literally: the transferred amount, 700, is also A2's
    new balance. The test checks the exact response body instead.
  - ACC-AC14 accepts either order of the two concurrent requests, as the AC
    allows, but nothing forces both orders to happen in a run.
  - A few tests (LED-AC07/09/10, REV-AC01/02/07/22, SYS-AC11/25) check
    settlement balances or reconcile across the whole database. They are
    reliable in the normal one-file-at-a-time run, but could fail if several
    test runs share the database at once.
  - A request with an unsupported media type (415) or an oversized body (413)
    still answers 500 until 09-hardening adds those error types.
  - ADR-0011's 08-api follow-up (the OpenAPI description of minor units) is now
    done, but no task asked me to close it, so I didn't mark it.
  - The OpenAPI document is served at /docs/json; there is no copy in docs/api/,
    although AGENTS.md lists that folder.

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  I found no Critical, High or Medium defects on the branch or in the uncommitted work. There are four Low findings, two in failure handling and two in the docs.

     Every acceptance criterion assigned to 08-api in the plans has a ticked task and a test with its ID in the name. The one ticked AC without a test is LED-AC17, which is proven by a CI step instead. All 08-api tasks are ticked. I could not run the tests or npm run trace, because this review was read-only.

     1. Low: database connection-loss warnings have no reqId
     - Where: src/app.ts:128 (passes the root app.log to the pool), which logs at src/platform/db/database.ts:75.
     - What is wrong: when a connection that a request holds is lost, the pool writes the "database connection lost" warning with the root logger, so the line has no correlation id. SYS-R22 and SEC-R21 require reqId on every line written while a request is handled.
     - How to trigger: run pg_terminate_backend on the backend of a deposit that is waiting for an account lock. The warning has no reqId; only the 500 line logged later has one.
     - Impact: in an incident, the connection loss cannot be tied to the request it broke.
     - Fix: log the loss through the request's logger, for example by having TransactionRunner.run report connection.broken to a logger passed in by the caller. Alternatively, record in SYS-R22 that pool-level lines are exempt.
     - AC: SYS-R22 and SEC-R21; no AC covers this case (SEC-AC16 and SYS-AC18 only exercise the normal path).

     2. Low: a client that aborts mid-body is logged as a 500
     - Where: src/platform/http/error-handler.ts:134 (the fallback after BODY_PARSE_ERRORS, line 86).
     - What is wrong: when the request stream errors before the body ends, Fastify passes the error to the error handler with statusCode 400. Its code is not one of the three parse codes, so toProblem turns it into 500 /problems/internal-error, and logFailure writes an error-level "request failed" line. The unit test in test/unit/http/error-handler.test.ts ("nothing else of Fastify") fixes this behaviour in place.
     - How to trigger: send a POST to /v1/accounts/{id}/withdrawals with Content-Length: 1000, write 10 bytes, then close the socket.
     - Impact: a client disconnect looks like a server defect, and will inflate the 500 metrics added in 09-hardening.
     - Fix: map unrecognised Fastify errors whose statusCode is 4xx to 400 /problems/malformed-request without the error log, or skip logging when request.raw.destroyed is true. 413 and 415 keep their own planned mappings.
     - AC: SYS-R25 and SYS-R34; missing AC.

     3. Low: the AI usage log has no row for the part 2 transcript
     - Where: docs/ai/README.md:35 (the last row is part 1).
     - What is wrong: docs/ai/transcripts/08-api-part2.txt exists but has no row. AGENTS.md §9 and the /ship checklist require one per transcript.
     - How to trigger: run /ship; its "every transcript has a row" check flags it.
     - Impact: the AI usage log is incomplete.
     - Fix: add the "08 part 2" row.
     - AC: missing AC (AGENTS.md §9).

     4. Low: the repository map promises a docs/api/ folder that does not exist
     - Where: AGENTS.md:104, and docs/dependencies.md:44.
     - What is wrong: the map lists docs/api/ for OpenAPI, but no plan creates it; the document is generated from the route schemas at /docs/json (src/platform/http/docs.ts). @redocly/cli is listed as "OpenAPI linting and bundling, first used in 08-api", but no script uses it.
     - How to trigger: look for docs/api/ or for a script that runs Redocly; neither exists.
     - Impact: AGENTS.md and the dependency list disagree with the code.
     - Fix: point the map at /docs/json and docs.ts, or add an export plus a redocly lint step; otherwise change the "first used in" phase of @redocly/cli.
     - AC: missing AC (AGENTS.md §10, definition of done).

     The files to change for these follow-ups are:
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/src/platform/http/error-handler.ts
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/src/platform/db/database.ts
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/src/app.ts
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/docs/ai/README.md
     - /Users/matiastorsello/supercool-finances/AGENTS.md

     Deferred, already planned: 413/415 mappings and the 16384-byte body limit (until then those requests answer 500; SEC-AC07 to SEC-AC09, 09-hardening); DB_POOL_MAX, the pool acquire timeout and PoolAcquireTimeout (SEC-AC27, SEC-AC28); request timeout and the SEC-R35 budget (SEC-AC25, SEC-AC38); graceful shutdown with close-with-grace, including Fastify's non-problem 503 while closing (SEC-AC20, SEC-AC21); log redaction (SEC-AC17); per-user rate limit, security headers and the /docs CSP, CORS and trusted proxies (SEC-AC02, SEC-AC03, SEC-AC11 to SEC-AC14); readiness and metrics, with SYS-AC02 and SYS-AC29 (09-hardening); MOV-AC14 and LED-AC15 (09-hardening); OpenAPI text for Idempotency-Key, the TTL and retry advice (spec 005, 09-hardening); movement and reversal OpenAPI text (specs 003 and 004, 09-hardening) and the bearer scheme with 401/403 responses (spec 006, 12-infra); SYS-AC14 and SYS-AC17 (11-e2e).

       error-level line. Because Fastify writes no "request completed" line once

      120 +function problemResponses(types: readonly DocumentedProblemTyp
          +e[]): Record<string, Response> {                              
      121 +  const byStatus = new Map<number, DocumentedProblemType[]>();
      122 +  for (const type of new Set([...types, ...SHARED_PROBLEMS])) 
          +{                                                             
      123 +    for (const status of PROBLEM_REFERENCE[type].statuses) {  
      124 +      byStatus.set(status, [...(byStatus.get(status) ?? []), t
          +ype]);                                                        
      125 +    }                                                         
      126 +  }                                                           
      127 +  const responses: Record<string, Response> = {};             
      128 +  for (const [status, statusTypes] of byStatus) {             
      129 +    const headers: Record<string, Header> = { 'X-Request-Id': 
          +REQUEST_ID_HEADER };                                          
      130 +    const retried = statusTypes.filter((type) => RETRIED.has(t
          +ype));                                                        
      131 +    if (retried.length > 0) {                                 
      132 +      headers['Retry-After'] = header(                        
      133 +        `Seconds to wait before retrying, sent with ${retried.
          +map((type) => `\`${type}\``).join(' and ')}. ${RETRY_POLICY}`,
      134 +        '1',                                                  
      135 +      );                                                      
      136 +    }                                                         
      137 +    if (status === 401) {                                     
      138 +      headers['WWW-Authenticate'] = header(

      140 +        'Bearer realm="supercool-finances"',                  
      141 +      );                                                      
      142 +    }                                                         
      143 +    responses[String(status)] = {                             
      144 +      description: [                                          
      145 +        `${STATUS_CODES[status] ?? String(status)}, as problem
          + details.`,

          +EFERENCE[type].when}`),

      159 +      },                                                      
      160 +    };                                                        
      161 +  }                                                           
      162 +  return responses;                                           
      163 +}

      165 +/** Completes an operation generated from its route schemas wi
          +th what its docs say. */                                      
      166 +function completed(operation: Operation, docs: OperationDocs, 
          +ttlSeconds: number): Operation {                              
      167 +  const name = operation.operationId ?? 'operation';          
      168 +  const parameters: Parameter[] = (operation.parameters ?? [])
          +.map((parameter) => {                                         
      169 +    const described = docs.parameters[parameter.name];        
      170 +    if (described === undefined) throw new Error(`${name}: no 
          +description of ${parameter.name}`);                           
      171 +    return { ...parameter, ...described };                    
      172 +  });                                                         
      173 +  if (docs.idempotencyKey !== 'none') {                       
      174 +    parameters.push({                                         
      175 +      in: 'header',                                           
      176 +      name: 'Idempotency-Key',                                
      177 +      required: docs.idempotencyKey === 'required',           
      178 +      schema: { type: 'string', minLength: 1, maxLength: 255, 
          +pattern: '^[!-~]+$' },                                        
      179 +      description: idempotencyKeyDescription(ttlSeconds, docs.
          +idempotencyKey),                                              
      180 +      example: '5b0d7f1e-6c2a-4e8b-9f3d-2a1c4e6b8d0f',        
      181 +    });                                                       
      182 +  }                                                           
      183 +  parameters.push({                                           
      184 +    in: 'header',                                             
      185 +    name: 'X-Request-Id',                                     
      186 +    required: false,                                          
      187 +    schema: { type: 'string', pattern: '^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128
          +}$' },                                                        
      188 +    description:                                              
      189 +      'An optional correlation id, returned in `X-Request-Id`,
          + written on every log line of the request and in a problem bod
          +y; any other value is replaced by a generated UUIDv7.',       
      190 +    example: 'checkout-7f3a',                                 
      191 +  });                                                         
      192 +                                                              
      193 +  let requestBody = operation.requestBody;                    
      194 +  if (requestBody !== undefined) {                            
      195 +    if (docs.requestBody === undefined) throw new Error(`${nam
          +e}: no description of the body`);                             
      196 +    const { description: text, example, required } = docs.requ
          +estBody;                                                      
      197 +    requestBody = {                                           
      198 +      ...requestBody,                                         
      199 +      required,                                               
      200 +      description: text,                                      
      201 +      content: Object.fromEntries(                            
      202 +        Object.entries(requestBody.content).map(([type, media]
          +) => [type, { ...media, example }]),                          
      203 +      ),                                                      
      204 +    };                                                        
      205 +  }                                                           
      206 +                                                              
      207 +  const status = String(docs.success.status);                 
      208 +  const success = operation.responses[status];                
      209 +  if (success === undefined) throw new Error(`${name}: no ${st
          +atus} response`);                                             
      210 +  const successHeaders: Record<string, Header> = { 'X-Request-
          +Id': REQUEST_ID_HEADER };                                     
      211 +  if (docs.success.status === 201) {                          
      212 +    successHeaders['Location'] = header(                      
      213 +      'The path of the created resource, under `/v1`.',       
      214 +      docs.success.location,                                  
      215 +    );                                                        
      216 +  }                                                           
      217 +  if (docs.idempotencyKey !== 'none') {                       
      218 +    successHeaders['Idempotent-Replayed'] = header(           
      219 +      'Present, with value `true`, only when the response is t
          +he stored response of an earlier request with the same `Idempo
          +tency-Key`.',                                                 
      220 +      'true',                                                 
      221 +    );                                                        
      222 +  }                                                           
      223 +  const responses: Record<string, Response> = {               
      224 +    [status]: {                                               
      225 +      description: docs.success.description,                  
      226 +      headers: successHeaders,                                
      227 +      content: Object.fromEntries(                            
      228 +        Object.entries(success.content ?? {}).map(([type, medi
          +a]) => [                                                      
      229 +          type,                                               
      230 +          { ...media, example: docs.success.example },        
      231 +        ]),                                                   
      232 +      ),                                                      
      233 +    },                                                        
      234 +    ...problemResponses(docs.problems),                       
      235 +  };                                                          
      236 +                                                              
      237 +  return {                                                    
      238 +    ...operation,                                             
      239 +    parameters,                                               
      240 +    ...(requestBody === undefined ? {} : { requestBody }),    
      241 +    responses: Object.fromEntries(                            
      242 +      Object.entries(responses).sort(([a], [b]) => a.localeCom
          +pare(b)),                                                     
      243 +    ),                                                        
      244 +    security: [{ bearer: [] }],                               
      245 +  };                                                          
      246 +}

⏺ The OpenAPI document is finished and exported to docs/api/openapi.yaml. npm 
  run openapi:lint passes with 0 errors and one warning, info-license, because
  the repo has no license; I left it rather than invent one.

  npm run check passes (283 unit tests), npm run test:integration passes (111
  files, 274 tests), and npm run trace -- --require unit,integration exits 0.
  Nothing is committed.

  What changed
  - Generation: the document is still generated from the route schemas with
    @fastify/swagger, and /docs and /docs/json are served as before. After
    generation, each operation is completed with its description, examples,
    headers and problem responses.
    - Shared reference text (every problem type, the Idempotency-Key header and
      the retry policy) is in a new src/platform/http/api-reference.ts.
    - The per-operation text is in the accounts and movements openapi.ts files.
    - An operation with no docs, or a parameter with no description, stops the
      document from being generated, so a new route can't go undocumented.
  - Contents:
    - Every request body, parameter and response has a description and an
      example. Every operation uses the bearer scheme.
    - The Idempotency-Key header is required on movements and reversals and
      optional on account creation. Its description gives the TTL (24 hours by
      default), the replay, key-reuse and in-progress answers, and the retry
      policy of spec 008 §1.5. Success responses also document X-Request-Id and
      Idempotent-Replayed.
    - Each operation lists the problem statuses it can answer, with an example
      body for every type. All 23 problem types of the specs are covered, and
      Retry-After and WWW-Authenticate are documented where they are sent.
  - Export: npm run openapi:export writes docs/api/openapi.yaml. It uses fixed
    placeholder settings and the default config values, so the file is the same
    in every environment. The YAML comes from @fastify/swagger itself, so no new
    dependency was needed.
  - Lint: npm run openapi:lint runs redocly lint docs/api/openapi.yaml. I added
    the generated file to .prettierignore.
  - Tests: test/unit/openapi.test.ts fails when the committed YAML differs from
    what the routes generate; I checked that it fails on an edited file. It also
    checks that every request and response has a description and an example,
    and that every problem type appears.
  - CI: the ci job has a step whose run is exactly npm run openapi:lint, right
    after npm run check.
  - Docs: AGENTS.md has rows for the two new commands and an updated docs/api/
    entry in the repository map. The README's API section points to the YAML
    file.

  Before you ship
  - Not-yet-served types: the document includes the four problem types the
    service doesn't answer yet: 413 payload-too-large, 415
    unsupported-media-type and 429 rate-limited arrive in 09-hardening, and
    upstream-unavailable comes from nginx. Until then the service answers 413
    and 415 cases with 500. No spec fixes their titles and detail texts, so I
    wrote them. A test makes sure every type the service already answers matches
    its registry entry, and it will catch a mismatch for these four once
    09-hardening adds them.
  - No AC covers this: the export, the lint and the CI step are tooling with no
    acceptance criterion behind them, so the new tests are named after
    requirement SEC-R44.
  - Tasks left unticked: this work overlaps the OpenAPI parts of the docs tasks
    of specs 003, 004, 005 and 006 and the 413/415/429 responses of spec 007,
    but those tasks belong to later phases and also cover README sections,
    runbooks and ADR follow-ups.

✻ Cooked for 38s · done 6:06 PM

❯ /audit                                                                        
  ⎿  No Critical, High or Medium findings: the money, locking, idempotency and authorization paths all match specs 003 and 005. The five findings are Low, and four are in the uncommitted OpenAPI work. I did not run the test suites or npm run trace, because they write report files and this review is read-only. Instead I checked by hand that every AC named by a ticked task has a test whose name contains its ID. All do except LED-AC17, which CI proves by running npm run reconcile rather than by a test.

     Findings

     1. Low: the OpenAPI document lists answers the service does not give yet, and the export tooling has no task
     - src/platform/http/api-reference.ts:14-36, :89; src/modules/accounts/adapters/http/openapi.ts:158-164; src/modules/movements/adapters/http/openapi.ts:284-291; package.json:33-34; .github/workflows/ci.yml:64
     - What is wrong: the document now gives every operation 413 /problems/payload-too-large, 415 /problems/unsupported-media-type and 429 /problems/rate-limited. It also says a 503 covers "no database connection free". None of these exist in the service yet; the 413/415 mappings, the rate limit and the pool timeout belong to 09-hardening. Plan 007 schedules the OpenAPI 413/415/429/503 responses for 12-infra. Separately, no task covers docs/api/openapi.yaml, npm run openapi:export, npm run openapi:lint, the drift test or the new CI step, which goes against the AGENTS.md rule that a task exists before the code.
     - How to trigger: send POST /v1/accounts/{id}/withdrawals with Content-Type: text/plain. The service answers 500 /problems/internal-error and logs an error, while the document promises 415.
     - Impact: a client built from the document will not handle the 500 it actually gets. The two docs tasks (12-infra and this branch) now overlap.
     - Suggested fix: either mark the edge types as "answered from 09-hardening" in their when text, or leave them out of the per-operation responses until the mappings exist. Add an 08-api task to specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md for the export, lint and drift test.
     - AC: missing AC (SEC-R44 only requires the endpoints to be listed under /v1).

     2. Low: a /v1 route without docs breaks /docs/json on first request, not at startup
     - src/platform/http/docs.ts:877-881
     - What is wrong: transformObject throws for any /v1 operation without OperationDocs. @fastify/swagger builds the document only on the first app.swagger() call, so the error surfaces then, as a 500, not when the app starts.
     - How to trigger: call GET /docs/json on the test app. Its throwing-route seam (GET /v1/test/throw, test/support/test-app.ts:35) has no docs, so the call fails with "no OpenAPI docs for GET /v1/test/throw".
     - Impact: the docs are broken in the test app today. In production a future undocumented route would make /docs/json answer 500; only the unit test in test/unit/openapi.test.ts guards against that.
     - Suggested fix: give the throwing route schema: { hide: true }. Build the document in an onReady hook (call app.swagger() once) so a missing doc fails at startup.
     - AC: SEC-R44.

     3. Low: two drifts in the document's text
     - src/platform/http/docs.ts:730-732, src/platform/http/api-reference.ts:73-74
     - What is wrong:
       - Idempotent-Replayed is documented only on the success response. A replayed 404, 409 or 422 rejection also carries it (IDM-R07, IDM-AC15, IDM-AC16).
       - The text for /problems/account-not-active says "An account the movement debits or credits is frozen or closed". Another customer's frozen or closed destination actually answers /problems/destination-unavailable (MOV-R15, ACC-R19).
     - How to trigger: replay a withdrawal stored with 422 /problems/insufficient-funds. The response has Idempotent-Replayed: true, but the 422 response in the document does not list that header.
     - Impact: client authors may branch on the wrong header or problem type.
     - Suggested fix: add the IdempotentReplayed header reference to the stored-rejection statuses of keyed operations (404, 409, 422), and limit the account-not-active text to the caller's own accounts.
     - AC: IDM-R07, MOV-R15 (docs).

     4. Low: AGENTS.md says the committed openapi.yaml is the document /docs/json serves, but they can differ
     - AGENTS.md (openapi:export row), scripts/openapi.ts:13-22
     - What is wrong: the served document includes the running values of MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR and IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS. The export always uses the defaults.
     - How to trigger: start the service with IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS=3600. /docs/json says 1 hour; docs/api/openapi.yaml says 24 hours.
     - Impact: small documentation inaccuracy.
     - Suggested fix: in AGENTS.md and the README, say the committed file is generated with the default settings.
     - AC: missing AC (docs).

     5. Low: a misplaced doc comment in sqlstate.ts
     - src/platform/db/sqlstate.ts:37-41
     - What is wrong: the new sqlstateOf was inserted between isRetryable and its doc comment. isRetryable lost its comment, and sqlstateOf now has two stacked comments, the first of which describes isRetryable.
     - How to trigger: read the file, or hover sqlstateOf in an editor.
     - Impact: misleading documentation only.
     - Suggested fix: move the "A deadlock or serialization failure…" comment down to isRetryable.
     - AC: none.

     Deferred, already planned: the 413/415 mappings and body limits, the per-user rate limit and 429, graceful shutdown, pool sizing and acquire timeout, the request timeout and timeout budget (SEC-R33, SEC-R35), the 503 for a statement timeout, log redaction (SEC-AC17), security headers, CORS and trusted proxies (all 007 and 000 tasks for 09-hardening); nginx and load-balancer timeouts and limits (10-runtime); replica and load ACs (11-e2e); the OpenAPI 413/415/429/503 responses and README updates (12-infra).

     Categories without findings

     - Spec conformance: every 08-api task is ticked and every AC it names has a test with its ID; the order of checks (SYS-R31), roles, the 404 for foreign or non-UUID ids, and the /v1 prefix all match the specs.
     - Money: no number or float on amounts; the API takes digit strings, converted with BigInt and capped at MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR; the presenters use bigint.toString().
     - Transactions and concurrency: one unit of work per movement; the key row is the first write, then the savepoint, then lock_timeout set right before the first FOR UPDATE in ascending order; system accounts are never locked; status and balance are checked only after the locks; retries are bounded on 40001 and 40P01; no in-process state beyond per-request data.
     - Idempotency: the key row is claimed first; a replay returns the stored status, headers and exact bytes before validation; another fingerprint gives 422; business rejections are rolled back to the savepoint and stored; validation errors, 500s and 503s are not stored; the key wait answers 409 and the account lock timeout 503, as separate settings.
     - Security: authentication and the role check run at onRequest before the body is read; schemas are strict; foreign or system resources answer the same 404; SQL is parameterized; logs carry no tokens or query strings; the new transcripts contain only test placeholder secrets.
     - Failure handling: every error is answered as application/problem+json; the rest of this category is deferred to 09-hardening.
     - Tests: lock waits are observed with pg_blocking_pids polling rather than fixed sleeps; integration files run one at a time; the invariant ACs (overdraw, deadlock, reconciliation) are covered.

     Follow-ups: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
     Verdict: ready to ship

⏺ All five follow-ups are fixed. npm run check passes (283 unit tests), npm run 
  openapi:lint gives 0 errors and the same license warning, npm run 
  test:integration passes (111 files, 275 tests), and npm run trace -- --require
  unit,integration exits 0. I regenerated docs/api/openapi.yaml with npm run 
  openapi:export. Nothing is committed.

  1. Only what the service answers today:
     - The document no longer lists payload-too-large (413),
       unsupported-media-type (415), rate-limited (429) or upstream-unavailable
       (502, 503, 504), in the info table or in any operation. The 503 text no
       longer mentions "no database connection free"; the YAML has none of these
       strings.
     - test/unit/openapi.test.ts now checks that these four types, the 413, 415,
       429, 502 and 504 responses and that phrase are absent, with a comment
       pointing to the 12-infra docs task of plan 007.
     - specs/007-security-ops/tasks.md has a new ticked 08-api task for the YAML
       export, openapi:export, openapi:lint, the drift test and the CI step.
  2. Startup failure:
     - The throwing-route seam is now hidden from the document.
     - The document is built once in an onReady hook, so an undocumented /v1
       route stops the app from starting. I checked this with a throwaway route:
       startup failed with "no OpenAPI docs for GET /v1/undocumented". The hook
       is added only after the swagger plugin has loaded, because swagger()
       fails if it runs before the plugin's own onReady hook.
     - test/integration/security/api-docs.test.ts now checks that the test app
       serves /docs/json without the throwing route.
  3. Header and wording: keyed operations now list Idempotent-Replayed on their
     404, 409 and 422 responses, the statuses of stored rejections. The
     account-not-active text is limited to the caller's own accounts and says
     that another customer's frozen or closed destination answers
     destination-unavailable.
  4. Defaults vs running values: AGENTS.md and the README now say that
     docs/api/openapi.yaml is generated with the default settings, while
     /docs/json shows the running values of MAX_AMOUNT_MINOR and
     IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_TTL_SECONDS.
  5. sqlstate.ts: the "A deadlock or serialization failure…" comment is back on
     isRetryable.

  One mismatch I left because you didn't list it: the 503 service-unavailable
  text still mentions "a request timeout", and the request timeout only arrives
  in 09-hardening. A statement timeout already answers 503 today, so that part
  is accurate. If you want the document to match today's service exactly, I'll
  drop "request".

  The finding: three generic-api-key hits, all on the same value. It is the
  example Idempotency-Key UUID 5b0d7f1e-6c2a-4e8b-9f3d-2a1c4e6b8d0f. It sits at
  src/platform/http/docs.ts:116 (keyExample), and it is repeated at

  findings.

  wouldn't help. These are the ways out:

    example, such as 11111111-2222-4333-8444-555555555555, then regenerate
    docs/api/openapi.yaml. The transcript copies would still need redacting.

    from the checkout root, with no --config.
  - /ship: runs gitleaks git --no-banner --redact --log-opts="origin/main..HEAD"
    from the repository root, with no --config.

  either.
